- [~] **Q1655 LOW email-unsubscribe performs the write on a GET, now for job-match mail too (lh-authz-rls review of Q1585, 2026-10-12; inherited from the promotions flow).** supabase/functions/email-unsubscribe/index.ts writes before any method check, and the job-match List-Unsubscribe URL is header-only, so any client or scanner that GETs it turns off that member's new-job emails. RFC 8058 clients POST. Fix: GET renders a confirm page that POSTs; POST (One-Click) writes. Same file: the resubscribe confirmation pages (promotions and job_matches) are unreachable because every POST returns text first. finding: lh-authz-rls@57aea077b#3 **BUILT 2026-10-17 (sec-c, ready/sec-c-1017-11; live proof owed after deploy; lead decision: build the web page):** a GET now writes nothing and does no read: it answers 303 to the new public web page /unsubscribe with the same e/sig/list (src/pages/auth/Unsubscribe.tsx, on the AuthShell card). That page asks "Turn off new-job emails?" / "Unsubscribe from promotional email?" with one primary button, POSTs `action=unsubscribe` and shows the result with a Resubscribe button (`action=resubscribe`). Page POSTs answer JSON; the RFC 8058 one-click POST (any other body) still writes and answers text; signature checking is unchanged. FOUND on the way (folded in here, not filed): the old HTML pages could never render, because Supabase serves an edge function's HTML as text/plain (MEASURED 2026-10-17: GET with an invalid token answered 400 `content-type: text/plain` + nosniff with the whole <!DOCTYPE html> page as text). So a member who clicked the footer link saw raw HTML source. NOT measured: the old 200 page for a real signed link. The poster test account gets no job-match email (send-notification-email: skipped test_recipient), and the temporary signing function that would have made a link was refused deployment by the permission system. GUARDS: src/test/edge/unsubscribeGetWritesNothing.test.ts (GET valid/forged -> 303 to the page with exactly e/sig/list and zero writes; page POSTs write and answer JSON; a bad signature writes nothing; one-click unchanged; RED on the old function 6 of 7, 4/4 mutates killed) and src/test/unsubscribePage.test.tsx (the page sends nothing until the button, reads success only from the matching JSON status, offers Resubscribe; 3/3 mutates killed). Done when, on prod, a GET of a real link writes nothing and lands on /unsubscribe, and its button turns the email off. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, src/pages/auth/Unsubscribe.tsx and src/test/edge/unsubscribeGetWritesNothing.test.ts are on origin/main; the live GET-writes-nothing proof on prod is still owed (no done-when marker).** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: partly done.** Left: On prod, prove a GET of a real unsubscribe link writes nothing and its button turns the email off.

**Done items leave this file (Q16, 2026-09-26):** a ticked `- [x]` item is moved
verbatim to the dated archive docs/archive/OPEN-done-YYYY-MM.md by
`npm run inventories:refresh` (scripts/archive-done.mjs); check:generated fails
while one is left here. The queue score, next free number and the done-item
guard check read this file AND the archives, so archiving never changes a count.

**`[~]` items say when they are done (2026-09-27):** put what is left as one or
more markers on the item, written out in full and ANDed together:
``done-when: sql `SELECT <one column>` => <expected>`` (read-only, first row),
`done-when: test <path>`, `done-when: issue #N closed`, or `done-when: pr #N merged`.
Only add markers when they cover everything that is left (a screenshot or a
review still to do means no marker yet). `node scripts/open-done-when.mjs` runs them;
the nightly `open-done-when` workflow opens one nightly-red issue listing the items
that are ready to tick. src/test/openPartlyDoneItemsSayDoneWhen.test.ts ratchets the
count of `[~]` items with no marker.

- [ ] **Q2493 MEDIUM The Helpr delivery lookup may put the buyer's typed address in logs and an ops alert (found 2026-10-30 by the address lookup lane; not measured).** supabase/functions/_shared/geocodeDropoff.ts builds `detail` from `String(e)` when a fetch throws, and declutter-checkout logs that detail and sends it in its ops alert. The address is in the request URL's query string, and a fetch error can quote the URL; whether Deno's does was not measured. The new geocode-address path carries only a status. done-when: geocodeDropoff.ts keeps only the error's name and status, with a test that a thrown error quoting the URL never reaches `detail`. Left alone here because declutter-checkout is a money function.
- [ ] **Q2403 LOW A Declutter checkout replaced because the setup fee changed is superseded in the database BEFORE its old Stripe page is expired, so a buyer who pays the old page in that gap is refunded as buyer_restarted_checkout and loses the card fee for a restart the system caused (lh-silent-failure review of the Q2352 rebase, 2026-10-26; code read of 20261026154738 declutter_start_checkout and declutter-checkout pay()).** The same order already exists for cart-line supersedes; the window is seconds. Fix: expire the old page first for a fee change too (the handover-change pre-check), or treat a payment on an order superseded for a fee change as superseded (full_platform_absorbs). GUARD when built: an edge case in src/test/edge/declutter-payments.test.ts that a fee-change supersede expires the old page before declutter_start_checkout replaces it. finding: lh-silent-failure@d0dff457f8#2 **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q2404 LOW The Declutter card fee on an order is Stripe's fee on sale + setup fee, so a seller-pays refund (seller_backed_out, seller_out) bills the seller the card cost of the $2 setup fee, a buyer-fault refund keeps it from the buyer, and on a fee owed back (refund_due) the platform absorbs Stripe's fee on the $2 (lh-money-escrow review of the Q2352 rebase, 2026-10-26; code read of 20261026154738 card_fee_cents).** Cents per order. Fix: split the card fee pro rata between the sale and the setup fee before declutter_record_refund uses it. GUARD when built: a PGlite step in src/test/pglite/declutterSetupFeeOnce.pglite.mjs that a seller_out refund on a fee-carrying order bills only the sale's share. finding: lh-money-escrow@d0dff457f8#2 **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q2405 LOW An enrolled test account pays the setup fee on a Declutter purchase (test key) but is never asked at a first listing: declutter_setup_fee_due_cents exempts nobody while declutter_setup_fee_before_list exempts admins and enrolled testers (lh-money-escrow review of the Q2352 rebase, 2026-10-26; code read).** Harmless on live money (test mode), but the two first-time steps disagree for testers. Fix: one carve-out for both, or none for both. GUARD when built: a pin in src/test/declutterSetupFeeOnce.test.ts that both read the same exemption. finding: lh-money-escrow@d0dff457f8#6 **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

5. **Push notifications (Q82): three things only you can do.** The code
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner pop-up): "cut it when main is current".** Claude runs fastlane beta from main
   once the open landings are in and main CI is green; you install it and open the app once.
   cause is fixed and pushed (see Q82), but no phone has the fix yet.
   (a) **Cut a TestFlight build from main** (at or after the Q82 fix commit)
   and install it on your iPhone. The only builds with the AppDelegate
   forwarding are ce4d38d59 (2026-09-02) and later, and every build so far
   also has the boot race (it depends on timing, so it may not hit every
   launch). (b) **On that build: sign in, go to Home, tap "Enable" on the
   notifications pill, then Allow.** The pill only shows after you've posted
   or applied, or on a later launch more than an hour after install. If you tapped "Don't
   Allow" on any earlier build, iOS will not ask again: turn it on in
   Settings > Helpr > Notifications first, then cold-launch the app. Within
   seconds `select count(*) from push_tokens` should be 1 and
   analytics_events should show `push_token_saved`. If it doesn't, the
   analytics events say which step failed. (c) **Only if you run the app
   straight from Xcode on your phone:** those builds get SANDBOX APNs tokens,
   and prod sends to the production APNs host (`APNS_USE_SANDBOX` is unset),
   so APNs rejects them with BadDeviceToken and the sender deletes the row.
   TestFlight/App Store builds are unaffected. Leave this alone unless you
   test from Xcode. APNs credentials are fine: APNS_KEY_ID, APNS_TEAM_ID
   (digest matches P85MCK558V), APNS_BUNDLE_ID (digest matches com.Helpr)
   and APNS_AUTH_KEY are all set in Supabase secrets. Whether the .p8 key
   itself is valid can't be checked without a real token to send to.

   On every push to main, Supabase's GitHub integration applies new migrations
   (and deploys edge functions) to prod within about a minute. Our deploy
   workflow's safety checks (lint, replay test, destructive-DDL stop) run after
   that, so for the last 24h (24 migrations) they have not blocked anything.
   Measured from prod's own logs 2026-09-23 (Q117). What you do: Supabase
   dashboard > Project Settings > Integrations > GitHub, and switch off
   automatic deploys to the production branch (exact toggle name not verified).
   Until then, each new migration turns db-deploy red by design (the new
   provenance check), so the side channel stays visible.
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-26 (owner asked "whats the best option?"): switch it OFF.** Measured: db-deploy
   36261725456 (18:16Z) judged 124 prod versions since the cutoff, 108 with a db-deploy receipt and 16
   acknowledged side-channel applies, the newest of them 2026-09-23 ~16:10Z (Q194). So nothing has come
   in by the side channel for about 3 days, but that is not proof the toggle is off. Off is the only
   setting where db-deploy's lint, replay and destructive-DDL checks actually run before prod changes.
   Still the owner's dashboard click; the provenance check stays as the detector either way.

7. **Backups (Q45): the restore is proven weekly now; five things are yours.**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-23 (owner pop-ups): (a) yes, Pro is paid and intended; (b) keep 14-day retention; (c) back up uploaded files EXCEPT id-documents, encrypted (work item Q147); (d) no PITR (no paid upgrade); (e) BACKUP_PASSPHRASE and ban_fingerprint_salt go in the owner's password manager (owner to-do).**
   Measured 2026-09-23. Nothing below is needed for the drill to stay green.
   (a) **Is the Supabase org really on Pro?** The API says `plan: "pro"`, and
   `supabase backups list` shows 7 daily platform backups. Every doc said
   "free tier". If you are paying $25/mo, you already have in-place daily
   restores for 7 days. If you did not mean to be on Pro, that is a bill.
   (b) **Backup retention is 14 days, not 90.** The repo caps artifacts and
   logs at 14 days (`maximum_allowed_days` 90). Raising it to 90 is one
   setting, but it lengthens EVERY workflow's artifacts, and on the free
   GitHub plan that can go past the artifact-storage quota (billed). The
   alternative is a monthly copy somewhere else. Your call.
   (c) **Storage files (photos, ID documents) are in NO backup**: 152 objects,
   about 10 MB (proof-photos 64, job-photos 61, message-attachments 18,
   user-documents 4, avatars 4, id-documents 1). The platform backup excludes
   them too. Backing them up needs a service-role key as an Actions secret and
   puts ID documents (encrypted) into a GitHub artifact: a privacy trade-off.
   (d) **PITR** (restore to the minute) is a paid add-on, about $100/mo plus
   Small compute. Without it, worst-case loss is about 24 h (platform) or
   24 h + GitHub's up-to-7 h schedule delay (ours). You said no paid upgrades,
   so this is only the measured gap.
   (e) **Two secrets that no backup holds.** `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE`: is there a
   copy outside GitHub? If not, losing the GitHub account also loses every
   backup. `ban_fingerprint_salt` (vault): it cannot be re-created, and
   without it every stored ban fingerprint stops matching after a restore.
   Want it (encrypted) in the backup, or kept in your password manager?

    36230166945: /admin?view=jobs alone took 97 min (412 presses), mostly the same four controls on every job
    card. Landed without asking: shards now claim rows from one shared queue (Q725), so a heavy row no longer
    strands the rest of its shard. Still yours: (A) keep pressing every card (complete, slow; ~97 min a run on
    that view), or (B) press every control on the first N cards of a repeated list and only count the rest
    (much faster; a defect that shows only on card N+1 would be missed). Default until answered: (A).
31. **Group crews: three money rules (Q728/Q729, 2026-09-27).** Group jobs are off
    (GROUP_JOBS_ENABLED=false), so nothing is charged under these yet. (A) CREW_FEE_PAYS_UNCONFIRMED:
    a late-cancel fee is shared by a member the poster OFFERED the job to but who never confirmed.
    Built as yes (your Q407(13) answer); confirm. (B) CREW_COMPLETES_WHEN_HIRED_DONE: an under-filled
    crew completes when every hired member is done, and the unfilled shares go back to the poster.
    Built as yes; confirm. (C) New: when a crew dispute or an unfilled slot returns a member's share,
    only the share itself (budget + urgent fee) goes back. The platform fee, sales tax and any gift
    card on that charge are NOT returned. Should they be returned in proportion to the share?
    ANSWERED 2026-09-27: yes, pro-rata. BUILT (see the ANSWERED block above); guard src/test/edge/process-scheduled-payouts-crew.test.ts.

## FEEDS — mirrored from the alert ledger, nightly-red issues and the audit bus (node scripts/open-sync-trackers.mjs)

Each item mirrors one open problem; its `feed:` tags are sticky (never edit them) and its `done-when:` holds once the source closes, when the sync flips it to `[~]`. The same problem seen again while its item is open is attached to that item (marked Seen again), never filed twice; every red overnight check is one sub-line of the ONE Fix the test robots item, which the sync rewrites.

- [~] **Q865 MEDIUM** (nightly-red #1754 prod-audit, 2026-09-30): 156 of run 36766443019's 158 skips are NOT justified. deep-links, messy-input and interruptions call `ensureFundedOpenJob` in `beforeAll`; under live Stripe it calls `skipLivePay` (`test.skip`), which in a `beforeAll` skips EVERY test in the file, including those that never touch the funded job (signed-out redirects, gone-job, /user/:id, the six /profile?tab= links). Only tests that read `fx.openJob` should skip; they already carry per-test `test.skip(!fx.openJob, …)`. The other 2 skips (expanding-search) are justified. Fix: let the fixture return "none" in live mode instead of skipping, keep the live-pay skip per test, and GUARD the class: a `beforeAll` must not call a fixture that can `test.skip` (static scan of e2e/prod-audit, @mutate one spec back). Then re-measure `prod-audit.perTest`: set to 138.2 (the max of runs 36744778439 = 138.2 and 36766443019 = 137.7, both with 91 tests run) so the budget step passes today. Run 36364639347 (before the switch, 124 tests) measured 109.9, which is inside the two-way band, so the un-skip will not trip it; lower it to the new measurement in the same commit. done-when: issue #2245 closed (tick when a prod-audit run shows only justified skips, and `prod-audit.perTest` equals its measured value). **Fixed 2026-09-30 (Q865 lane):** the three beforeAll hooks now mint inside `unlessLivePay(() => ...)` (e2e/prod-audit/fundedOpenJob.ts), which turns the live-pay skip into a value; only tests that open a funded fixture skip, per test, via `skipWhenUnfundedLive` -> `skipLivePay`. Of the 156 (`playwright test --list`): deep-links 18 of 21 un-skipped, interruptions 5 of 12 (the 7 apply tests still skip), messy-input 112 of 123 (4 applicant sweeps, 2 pending-applicant sweeps and 5 openJob/pending/disputed explores still skip); 135 un-skipped, 21 skip with the live-pay reason. Same mechanism as the owner's 10-09 21:45Z incident (commit 60de96aeb): getSession() returns null when the stored access token has expired and the refresh fails, and supabase-js then sends the anon key. 60de96aeb/63dc73e2a/24bed73f1 (landed 2026-10-11, not in either failing release d9342ecf/c123c618) stop the false sign-out but NOT the anon requests. Since 2026-10-10 14:39Z: 0 events (Sentry `message:*permission denied for*`, 7d, read 04:2xZ). The iOS burst 10-09 13:38-13:40Z ran 2.5 min across /home, /post-job, /profile, /messages, /jobs, so it is not only a sub-second wake race. Fix still to build (an auth change, so it carries a Sensitive check trailer): before a /rest/v1 or /functions/v1 request leaves with the anon bearer while auth-js still holds a session that failed to refresh on the network, wait for TOKEN_REFRESHED (auth-js caches a failed refresh for REFRESH_FAILURE_COOLDOWN_MS = 60 s, so a retry inside that window gets the cached failure) and send with the user's token, else send as today; guard on the real supabase-js client like src/lib/authSignOut.lateLogout.test.ts. **ONE ROOT CAUSE, MERGED 2026-10-14** (owner: items that share one root cause are one item). MEASURED 2026-10-14: (1) Sentry, 100 newest "permission denied for" events (14 d): 98 on iOS with NO Sentry user (src/main.tsx sets the user from the Supabase session, so no user = no session), on signed-in routes (/home, /posts, /jobs, /profile, /post-job, /messages), in bursts 2026-10-09 01:38Z, 02:48Z, 13:37-13:40Z, then 10-10 02:51Z/12:00Z/14:39Z and 10-13 22:57Z/23:01Z (profiles only); 2 on Mac OS with a user (10-09 21:45Z). (2) The "session lost on a signed-in screen" events (JAVASCRIPT-3H, iOS, no user) carry the SAME timestamps as the 10-10 and 10-13 profiles events; "missing authorization header" (JAVASCRIPT-35) and the real functionshttperror events (JAVASCRIPT-1Q, 10-09 02:48Z and 13:38Z, iOS, no user) sit inside the bursts (1Q's 10-12 events are automated, the test poster). The error-screen ledger rows for /posts and the Sentry "we couldn't load this" rows were first and last seen inside the same bursts. (3) Grants on prod: every table/function named is granted to `authenticated` and NOT to `anon` (applications, jobs: column SELECT for authenticated only; backout_notices, job_schedule_change_requests, referral_codes, login_history, recurring_visit_payments, payout_transfers, gift_cards: table SELECT for authenticated only; get_job_offer_targets, get_my_saved_helpers: EXECUTE for authenticated only; profiles: anon may SELECT but only authenticated may UPDATE, 87 columns). So the queries ran as anon. within 3 s of a Realtime tenant re-initialise, 10-25 01:08:13Z x1) are this same class: realtime.subscription_check_filters() only counts columns the subscriber's ROLE may SELECT, and `has_column_privilege` (live) is false for anon on jobs.helper_id and applications.helper_id (true for authenticated, true for anon on job_tracking), so a helper_id channel (useActivityData `activity-realtime`, userRealtimeBus applications:helper) joined with anon claims = the anon key, which supabase-js sends only when getSession() has no session. (b) The clearest repro yet, one iPhone (iOS 18.7, Safari web), 10-25 01:04-01:09Z: 4 failed password sign-ins (/auth/v1/token 400), Forgot password (/recover 200 at 01:07:29), the recovery link (/auth/v1/verify 303 at 01:08:12.198), GET /auth/v1/user authenticated at .658, then at .876-.893 FIVE signed-in reads left with NO JWT (profiles 200, user_blocks 200, user_roles 401, rpc/feature_switches_for_me 401, profiles 200) and at .894-.904 the SAME five again WITH the user JWT (all 200); the websocket opened at .896 and the helper_id filter error followed at 01:08:13.178. So on an auth-link landing one set of signed-in reads runs before the session is stored and is refused; the guard (src/lib/signedInRequestGuard.ts) cannot hold them because storage holds no refreshable session yet. The member saw no failure (the authenticated copies answered), only the Sentry/Postgres noise. (c) Item-6 Sentry issues read: JAVASCRIPT-3W (my_schedule_clashes / get_job_offer_targets, 10-19, a signed-in Sentry user on /home) and JAVASCRIPT-2T (recurring_visit_payments, last 10-22, screen /login, no Sentry user) are both EXECUTE/SELECT granted to authenticated only (proacl / grants, live), i.e. anon-bearer requests from signed-in screens: this item, not separate app bugs. - [~] **Q2409 MEDIUM cron http failure: refund-request-sweep and declutter-money returned 502 (2026-10-26).** From alert-ledger rows aeedd2495772 and 11f46545733f (error_logs: cron-http, 18:15Z). feed: ledger aeedd2495772. feed: ledger 11f46545733f. **ROOT CAUSE MEASURED 2026-10-27 (read-only):** function_edge_logs show the 502s (18:00:03Z refund-request-sweep, 18:08:00Z declutter-money) as text/html with no function version, no deployment id and no x-served-by: supabase-edge-runtime, in 69 ms and 44 ms; function_logs hold no boot for either at those moments; both answered 200 on every other run (next 18:10Z, 18:18Z). The platform gateway answered; our code never ran. Both functions were deployed from main 2026-10-26 02:08-02:10Z (v58, v19) and prod has applied none of the unlanded declutter branches' migrations (schema_migrations), so ready/declutter-refund-requests and ready/declutter-setup-fee-once are not involved. **FIXED (ready/cron-gateway-5xx-1027):** 20261027141932 gives a text/html 502/503 without the edge runtime's stamp, on a listed catch-up sweep (declutter-money, refund-request-sweep), the Q1690 rule: skipped only once the job's next run answers 2xx, filed if still failing at 30 minutes; 504, a function's own 5xx and every unlisted job are filed as before. Proof src/test/pglite/cronGateway5xxNeedsARepeat.pglite.mjs (ALL PASS 3x, 5 FAIL on main). - [~] **Q2410 MEDIUM /messages · undefined is not an object (evaluating 'e._result.default') (2026-10-25).** From alert-ledger rows 5a28db44e912 (user-error-screen, /messages, error_logs 2328bb32, 2026-10-25 22:13Z) and c5d2aafd1f77 (Sentry JAVASCRIPT-4J, /login, 2026-10-23, same TypeError). feed: ledger 5a28db44e912. feed: ledger c5d2aafd1f77. **ROOT CAUSE MEASURED 2026-10-27:** the /messages row's release 3caabd851f was mid prod-deploy (prod-deploy run 22:11:06-22:13:44Z); its component stack is Lazy > Suspense > SidePanelProvider, i.e. one of the seven shell lazies in App.tsx's nav Suspense, caught by the top-level ErrorBoundary. Vite 8.3.4's preload helper (read in node_modules) resolves a failed import() with undefined once main.tsx's vite:preloadError handler calls preventDefault() to start a recovery; React.lazy caches that and reads .default off it on every later render. The boundaries stayed quiet only while the recovery flag was up, so the same dead lazy surfacing after the flag dropped (a scheduled retry cancelled by navigating, or declined at fire time) was the generic card, a report, and a Try Again that re-threw. **FIXED (ready/lazy-poisoned-chunk-1027):** isChunkLoadError matches the poisoned-lazy read (WebKit, V8, Gecko, React-dev wordings), so every boundary recovers with a bounded reload or, when recovery is over, shows the honest card. Left: deploy, then close the two ledger rows. done-when: test src/test/errorBoundariesQuietDuringChunkRecovery.test.tsx **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: partly done.** Left: Close ledger rows 5a28db44e912 and c5d2aafd1f77.

(archive L2133) **OWNER DECIDED 2026-10-04 (pop-up, with Q1076): tests get ONE permanent test-only paid is_seed job set in the database (no real money, never shown to real users, excluded from payout jobs).** **SCOPED 2026-10-05 (alerts lane): this build is now the root cause of nightly-red #2245 (prod-audit, 21 of 25 failures), #2213 and #2294 (Q722 empty-account shapes), part of #2198, and so #2244.** The states the suites need between poster-e2e and helper-e2e: an open escrow job the Helpr has not applied to; accepted and confirmed but not on the way; on the way / in_progress; completed with no review; completed with a review the Helpr can answer; an unanswered dispute against the Helpr. The hard part is money: is_seed alone does not keep these rows away from auto-release-payment, process-scheduled-payouts, void-cancelled-payments, auto-resolve-disputes (a durable dispute would be auto-resolved and try a Stripe refund), charge-recurring-visits, auto-expire-jobs and money-reconciliation. That needs a dedicated fixture marker that every money cron and the reconciliation skip, a guard that derives the money crons and requires the skip, and an lh-money-escrow review. It is too large to start at the end of this session. Next: build it as one money-reviewed lane. **MERGED IN 2026-10-06 (triage):** Q941 (the 7 interruptions.spec.ts apply tests skip until the open un-applied escrow job exists; prod-audit.yml runs no seeder; still 7 skips on run 37270491218) and Q1076 (seed producers bypass accept_job/respond_to_direct_offer and leave states real jobs never reach; per the same 2026-10-04 decision they stamp states realistically). **MERGED IN 2026-10-06 (triage, second pass):** Q856 (shell-spacing reports messages@320-430 UNMEASURED until poster-e2e has two Active threads on live jobs). Q593 (a11y-prod's RESERVED accepted fixture) is the same build but stays its own item: it now mirrors nightly-red #2375. **DESIGN 2026-10-06 (e2e-reds lane; owner re-confirmed ONE permanent paid is_seed job, never public; lead: post-launch, both reviews before landing):** measured on prod 2026-10-06: anon open_jobs_browse = [] and 0 open/accepted/in_progress jobs with payment_status escrow (119 open abandoned seeds, 1 open unpaid, 1 accepted unpaid), so a11y-prod job-detail-1 skips UNJUSTIFIED (6 in run 37403676136) and prod-audit 37521973791 fails 16 tests + 7 unjustified skips on 'no in-progress job between the shared accounts'. (a) VISIBILITY: seed_jobs_hidden_publicly() is false on prod (feature_flags key unset), so an escrow seed would show on public Browse today (prod-seed.mjs:534 refuses escrow seeds for this reason), and flipping it true would also hide the row from helper-e2e. Migration: seed jobs visible only to viewers whose profiles.is_seed is true, on all four browse surfaces (open_jobs_browse, get_ranked_open_jobs, get_open_jobs_for_map, get_public_open_jobs): lh-authz-rls review. (b) UNHIREABLE: an escrow row with no PaymentIntent that press-every-control or a journey could apply to, hire and release would make release-payout transfer from the LIVE platform balance (no source_transaction). A fixture marker that apply_to_job / accept_application / release paths and every money cron listed above refuse, plus a guard deriving the money crons: lh-money-escrow review. (c) a service-role ensure script run by each workflow's fixtures job; a11y-prod authedScreens and the prod-audit gap lookups read the fixture id instead of open_jobs_browse. **BUILT 2026-10-07 (proof lane, owner decision 2026-10-07: ONE durable is_seed job funded in the database only, visible only to the test accounts, applied to and withdrawn from by 01-browse):** migration 20261007122020 adds public.test_fixture_jobs (server-only) and trg_jobs_test_fixture_never_hired, which for a listed job refuses, for every role, a Helpr, a direct offer, any status but open/cancelled and a payment_status move while open (PGlite src/test/pglite/testFixtureJobsCannotBeHired.pglite.mjs: 12 FAILED without it, ALL PASS with it, 3x replay); scripts/e2e/browseFixture.mjs keeps exactly one (ends an unusable open one before unregistering it); e2e/journeys/01-browse.spec.ts ensures it, applies, withdraws and cleans up in a finally. Guard: src/test/testFixtureJobsCannotBeHired.test.ts (6 @mutate, each red). lh-money-escrow review: SAFE WITH FIXES, both closed: (1) an unusable open fixture is ended before it is unregistered; (2) CI's poster is poster-e2e (71c56dfb), measured read-only: all 28 '[E2E DO NOT ACCEPT]' jobs created inside e2e-journeys 37552757280 + 37554735234's windows (00:35-01:47Z) and all 42 since 00:34Z are owned by 71c56dfb, none by 96c9899e; locally getSession('poster') mints poster-e2e too, so CI and local keep the same fixture. Still open in this item: the other suite states (accepted, in progress, completed, dispute: prod-audit / a11y / press), the /jobs:id + /user:id stall, press self-heal and the push 'Not Now' signal. **STATE FIXTURES BUILT 2026-10-12 (lane seed-fixtures, branch ready/seed-fixtures), applied to prod the same day:** scripts/e2e/seedStateFixtures.mjs (`--apply` idempotent, `--verify` read-only, `--teardown`, `--only=<key>`) keeps these poster-e2e jobs, is_seed, funded in the database only (escrow, no PaymentIntent, no Checkout Session), helper-e2e hired through the app's own RPCs: helper-scheduled-4d a87934d0-bb91-5215-b509-d49744cb2c5a and helper-scheduled-daybefore 9cd3fa38-7e53-57ca-813b-71bc15c8e389 (accepted; each with a 2-message thread; for Q1554, Q1561, Q593, Q1427), helper-arrived-photo 90aa6c8b-bf01-5430-ab29-ac182087ad5e (photo required, GPS-verified arrival, no before photo; Q1562), helper-completed c975aec8-ef5a-5fca-bac1-237320e7e557 (completed, payment_status released in the DB only; Q1561, work record), dispute-refiled a690c95f-ecd8-5791-86ce-b21b0c797674 (disputed, Helpr answered, withdrawn, re-filed; Q1327), crew-unconfirmed 0f2f7753-9390-57d1-8c4c-fcc84259bad3 (booked crew of helper-e2e + eli, neither confirmed; Q1402), and wipe row 2edc2b13-de6b-5d52-9528-c5fc168e4d81 in pre_verification_wipes for the incomplete account 60416646 (storage_done_at set, so the wipe sweep skips it; Q1286). Every child row id (applications, disputes, roster, messages) is printed by the script. NOT listed in test_fixture_jobs (its trigger refuses every status move, and a refusal inside a cron's set-based sweep would roll the sweep back for real users); the money safety is that every money mover refuses a job with no PaymentIntent before calling Stripe, the money crons skip is_seed, and no test account has a Stripe account. prod-seed.mjs never reviews or disputes them. (also: none found) **TRIAGE 2026-10-06 (e):** owner/dashboard-only check; not on the launch list. **MEASURED 2026-10-15 00:35Z (sec-a, from this Mac, no e2e workflow running):** one throwaway is_seed account (helpr-q1121-probe-20261015003509@mailinator.com, created by the admin API, deleted after: auth.users 0, profiles 0 re-read) got 60 wrong-password POST /auth/v1/token?grant_type=password in 6.5 s: 60 of 60 answered 400 `captcha_failed` ("captcha protection: request disallowed (no captcha_token found)"), 0 answered 429. So prod GoTrue has CAPTCHA ON for password sign-in: a script without a solved captcha never reaches the password check, which is the server-side backstop the client lockout needed. GoTrue's own per-IP sign-in limit could NOT be measured (the captcha check answers first; solving captchas from a script is not possible). Evidence ~/.lh-shots/sec-a-q1121/probe-20261015003509.json. Proposed guard (not built): a nightly live check that a captcha-less password sign-in still answers captcha_failed, so turning captcha off goes red. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, GoTrue's own per-IP limit still unmeasured; the proposed nightly captcha check is not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

Measured from code, not device: (a) on iOS `getLaunchUrl()` returns `ApplicationDelegateProxy.lastURL` and the cold-start `appUrlOpen` is retained until a listener attaches (node_modules/@capacitor/app AppPlugin.swift `retainUntilConsumed: true`), so a cold start from a link ran the handler TWICE (two Browser.close(), two navigate()); now the pair is collapsed (same URL, other source, within 10 s), while two real taps still route twice; (b) the appUrlOpen listener sat at the END of useNativePushSetup's one try, behind six push awaits, so any push throw left the process with no deep links; it now starts first on its own chain. AppDelegate.swift application(_:open:options:) and (_:continue:restorationHandler:) both forward to ApplicationDelegateProxy (read, correct; no UIScene manifest in Info.plist, so these are the entry points); Info.plist registers the `helpr` scheme. ANDROID: there is no `android/` project in the repo, so no intent-filter or launchMode exists to check — Android deep links cannot work until one is generated (`npx cap add android`) and given a `helpr` intent-filter + App Links; not done here. (d) An open review is not itself a payout block: if an admin releases the Q1324 system hold (or an admin hold pre-empted it), instant-payout, cash-out-credits and Stripe auto payouts read only payout_holds; make checkPayoutHold count an open review, or have admin_release_payout_hold refuse while one is open. (e) PRE-EXISTING, the larger one (lh-money-escrow re-review of f2644839e): a late cancellation pays a Helpr under review behind only the payout hold, and charges the poster. poster_cancel_job has no ban/review check and still charges the fee (cancellation_fee_percent(true, 1) = 50, measured live) and strikes the poster; void-cancelled-payments (single job and crew share), heldTipRepay and the chargeback pay-back claim through cancellation_fee_transfers or Stripe directly and check checkPayoutHold only. Fix: no fee and no strike when the Helpr (or a crew member) is under an open review or banned; a refusal trigger on cancellation_fee_transfers like refuse_payout_claim_while_held (or (d) above covers all hold-only paths at once). (b) a release refused by trg_refuse_payout_pending_during_ban_review answers neutral, final copy (create-payment edge case). (a) DONE too: auto-tip-charge ages out a tip held by an open review past its 14-day window like a payout hold (recorded 'failed', the poster told once, in words that never name the review; edge cases in src/test/edge/banReviewFreezesMoney.test.ts). STILL OPEN: (c) owner decision (refunds/money-in for a poster under review), (e) the poster is still charged the fee and struck when the Helpr is under review or banned (their pay itself now waits on the hold), **(f) DONE 2026-10-12 (branch ready/todo-a-5):** ban_review_retained_floor (20261013001155) returns the strictest standing retained judgment applied during the review (auto-applied, uncleared, a live retained row, recorded or re-applied since the review opened, not a spent suspension; a no-end suspension outlasts a dated one, in any row order), and BOTH admin_confirm_ban_settlement and lift_ban_settlement_review take it as a floor (a lift clears the card/bank matches first). PGlite src/test/pglite/banReviewConfirmStrictest.pglite.mjs on prod's bodies (md5 checked): live 6 FAIL, fixed ALL PASS 3x; guard src/test/banReviewConfirmKeepsStrictest.test.ts (every review decision that writes the standing takes the floor first, and the floor's filters). lh-authz-rls re-review: clean, with three LOW follow-ups kept here: (f1) the 'reapplied since' branch reads retained_bans.reapplied_at, stamped for ANY account, so a pardoned match whose retained row survived the unban (retained_via 'deletion', 1 on prod) can count if someone else re-applies that row during the review; fix: stamp the match row itself in enforce_retained_ban's ON CONFLICT flip and drop that branch; (f2) PRE-EXISTING: a lift whose result is 'banned' writes 'banned' over the review's 'banned', so no settlement trigger fires and the account's jobs stay unsettled once the freeze ends (a permanently_banned floor settles as of now, not as of the review); fix: run confirm's explicit settlement when a lift ends banned; (f3) PRE-EXISTING: confirm's rung branch still uses COALESCE(original_expires_at, '-infinity'), so a dated rung can replace a no-end review suspension. - [ ] **Q1727 MEDIUM The tax preview and the charge use different addresses (lh-money-escrow review of Q1578, 2026-10-12).** The preview prices the job ZIP (CheckoutStep.tsx ~166 -> calculate-tax ~120); Checkout taxes the poster's BILLING address (create-payment ~850 customer_update.address auto, no shipping address). Now that assembly/handyman are taxed (Q1578), a poster billed in another parish pays that parish's rate, and one billed out of state pays $0 with no alert (taxedZeroOnTaxableLouisianaLabor only fires for LA billing). Fix: give Stripe the job site as the tax address (Stripe prefers a shipping address), so preview == charge. GUARD when built: a create-payment edge case where billing != job ZIP and the session carries the job-site address, red first. finding: lh-money-escrow@6e679c20c#1 **TRIED 2026-10-12 (lane 1 money), NOT landed: the first design is unsafe.** Measured in Stripe test mode: a Customer's `shipping` beats its billing address for Checkout tax (billing Austin TX + shipping Delcambre LA taxed Louisiana). But writing the job site onto the shared Stripe Customer persists: Subscriptions and invoices also tax `customer.shipping` first, so a Pro/Plus subscription (txcd_10103001, taxable SaaS) and every renewal would be taxed at the last job site, and a ZIP outside Louisiana stamped as state LA may make Stripe refuse every automatic-tax checkout on that customer (customer_tax_location_invalid; unmeasured). Options for the next build: (a) Checkout without automatic_tax, with a precomputed "Sales tax" line from a tax.calculations call at the job site, committed with createFromCalculation on completion (what recurring visits already do); or (b) shipping_address_collection so the poster confirms the job address in Checkout. Either way, validate the ZIP is 700xx-714xx and send the same address shape to the preview (Q1657). **TRIED AGAIN 2026-10-12 (branch ready/todo-a-4), dropped before landing:** the build saved the job ZIP as customer.shipping before the session, i.e. the unsafe first design above; the lh-money-escrow review blocked it on the same grounds (create-pro-checkout taxes the same Customer with automatic_tax, so a Pro/Plus checkout and every renewal would carry the last job site; two overlapping checkouts can tax one job at the other's site). The next build is option (a) or (b) above. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, the tax address choice (a) or (b) is not built.**

- [~] **Q1737 MEDIUM A missing /assets/ or /fonts/ file now 404s, but the 404 carries `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (found proving Q1720, 2026-10-12).** MEASURED with curl -sI on www (before 21:33Z, build c4b1b5254): /assets/missing-Q1663-x.js, /assets/notificationFeed-B4je1ZJb.js and /fonts/missing-x.woff2 all answer 404 text/plain with that header, because vercel.json's `/assets/(.*)` and `/fonts/(.*)` header rules stamp every response under those paths, found or not. A browser may keep a 404 with explicit freshness, so a chunk asked for in a deploy's propagation window (Q199) could stay a cached 404 under that URL. Not reproduced in a browser. Fix to weigh: limit the immutable header to files that exist (e.g. `missing` on the header rule, or a 404 page with no-store). GUARD when built: extend src/test/immutablePathsNeverServeTheSpa.test.ts, plus a live curl in a post-deploy check. **BUILT 2026-10-17 (sec-c, ready/sec-c-1017-10; live proof owed after deploy):** vercel.json rewrites `/assets/(.*)` and `/fonts/(.*)` to the new api/missing-asset.ts (404 text/plain, `Cache-Control: no-store`). A rewrite never shadows a real file (filesystem first), and the function's own header beats the vercel.json rule: MEASURED on a throwaway Vercel PREVIEW (louisianahelpr-ny0sdqpp9, deleted after) carrying this exact headers/rewrites block, with `vercel curl -sI` and GET: /assets/real-Q1737.js and /fonts/real-Q1737.woff2 200 `public, max-age=31536000, immutable` (second GET x-vercel-cache HIT, still immutable); /assets/missing-Q1737-x.js, /assets/sub/dir/missing.css and /fonts/missing-x.woff2 404 `no-store`; /home 200 the SPA. Prod before (2026-10-17 ~10:00Z, scripts/check-missing-asset-headers.mjs against www): the three missing paths 404 with the immutable header, rc=1. GUARDS: src/test/immutablePathsNeverServeTheSpa.test.ts (every immutable prefix's missing files reach the no-store function first; the function's real 404; the live check's verdict red on the measured prod row and green on the measured preview, fail-closed on an empty read; 7/7 mutates killed) and a live step in prod-freshness.yml's served-commit job (scripts/check-missing-asset-headers.mjs, after prod serves the commit; it also requires a real hashed asset to stay immutable). - [ ] **Q1755 MEDIUM A test account can apply to (and be hired on) a REAL job, which puts a test account on real money (found by the lh-authz-rls and lh-money-escrow reviews of the test-mode branch, 2026-10-12; the 2026-10-09 incident was exactly this: the shared test Helpr applied to the owner's real job).** enforce_application_job_state rule C6 only stops a REAL user applying to a seed job (`v_job.is_seed AND seed_hidden_for(helper)`); nothing stops the reverse, nor a direct offer or accept between a real poster and a test account. With test mode for test accounts the money side is safe (a payout of live escrow to a test Helpr refuses as mixed_parties, and the poster's refund still runs in live mode), but such a job can never be paid out and needs a refund. Fix at the source: refuse a test account applying to, being offered or accepting a non-seed job (and a real account on a seed job, as today), in the trigger and the offer/accept RPCs. Data-model change: migration, PGlite 3x, lh-authz-rls review. GUARD when built: a PGlite test that a test-account application to a real job is refused, red on today's trigger. finding: lh-authz-rls@8e3b0e622#1 Note (lh-authz-rls review of 000ca9c1e, info #2, measured 2026-10-12: 57 test_accounts rows, all is_seed; besides 55 fixture emails they include the seed admin d3128db4 and apple-reviewer f2098932): the App Store review account pays on the TEST key, so on any REAL job it gets create-payment's 409 "This job pairs a test account with a real account"; App Review must be pointed at seed jobs. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, enforce_application_job_state on prod (SQL 2026-10-21) has no rule stopping a test account applying to a real job (only C6, the other way round).** **BUILT 2026-10-30 B84 (wip/b84-test-accounts-real-jobs, not landed): migration 20261030063902 adds one trigger function, enforce_test_account_off_real_jobs, on applications (INSERT), jobs (helper_id = every hire RPC, offered_to_helper_id = a direct offer; INSERT or UPDATE) and group_job_helpers (helper_id = a crew spot): a test_accounts member on a job whose is_seed is false is refused (42501 test_account_real_job). Its name sorts after trg_jobs_insert_column_lock and trg_jobs_seed_from_poster, so on INSERT it reads the final is_seed. Measured on prod first (read-only): 63 test accounts, all is_seed profiles; 461 jobs posted by test accounts, all is_seed; 0 applications, hires or direct offers pairing a test account with a non-seed job, so nothing existing is refused. A real member on a seed job keeps today's rule (C6, unchanged; conservative). GUARD: src/test/testAccountsOffRealJobs.test.ts (the three tables carry the trigger; it fires after every is_seed setter, inventory from source) + src/test/pglite/testAccountsOffRealJobs.pglite.mjs (16 PASS applied 3x; 6 FAIL with NEW_MIGRATION=skip). Needs lh-authz-rls review.**
  - Merged 2026-10-14 (same cause: a test account reaching real jobs): Q1758 is ticked into this item and keeps its full text. From Q1758: a mixed test/real cancelled job refunds the whole fee and leaves ledger rows pending; unreachable once this item lands.
- [ ] **Q1756 LOW Re-minting a test account's checkout leaves its old LIVE Checkout Session open until it expires (found by the lh-money-escrow review of the test-mode branch, 2026-10-12).** create-payment escrow skips reading a cs_live_ session on a job that now runs in test mode (the test key cannot read it) and mints a cs_test_ one, but does not expire the live one, which Stripe keeps payable for up to 24 hours. Measured 2026-10-12 by the reviewer: 0 of the 68 abandoned and 2 unpaid cs_live_ sessions on test accounts' jobs were ever paid; after deploy no new live session is minted for a test account. Expiring it needs the LIVE key on a test account's request (the one live call this design avoids); decide whether that is worth it. finding: lh-money-escrow@7389f2a30#6 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no change to create-payment for this; it is a decide-if-worth-it LOW (0 such sessions ever paid).** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

The Fastfile's metadata and release lanes stage that secret into the temporary upload copy only (stage_review_demo_password!), and ios-metadata.yml passes it; while the secret is unset the tracked file is still used. - [ ] **Q1791 MEDIUM Deleting an account never revokes its Sign in with Apple token (found preparing the 1.0.4 resubmission, 2026-10-13).** Apple requires an app offering Sign in with Apple to revoke the user's Apple tokens when the account is deleted (App Store Review 5.1.1(v)). Measured 2026-10-13: no call to appleid.apple.com/auth/revoke anywhere in supabase/ or src/, including supabase/functions/delete-own-account. Fix: keep the Apple refresh token at sign-in (server side), and have delete-own-account call /auth/revoke with a client secret signed by the Sign in with Apple key. Needs that key from the owner. GUARD when built: an edge test that deleting an Apple-linked account makes exactly one revoke call. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no call to appleid.apple.com/auth/revoke exists; Sign in with Apple is OFF for launch (Q1462, done), so this is needed only before Apple sign-in comes back.** **BUILT 2026-10-30 (wip/b83-apple-revoke, not landed): measured on prod first: Apple sign-in is back (16 apple identities), and we held NO Apple token anywhere (auth.identities.identity_data has only sub/email/name; auth.flow_state.provider_refresh_token empty on all 269 apple rows; no APPLE_SIGNIN_* secret, only APNs and MapKit keys). Code: migration 20261030050004 adds public.apple_sign_in_tokens (service role only, CASCADE); the iOS app sends the one-time Apple code to the new apple-token-store function, which trades it with Apple for the refresh token; accountPurge (run by all four delete paths before auth.admin.deleteUser) calls /auth/revoke once and drops the row, recorded as step apple_revoke but not blocking the deletion. GUARD: src/test/edge/appleSignInRevoke.test.ts (one revoke call per Apple-linked deletion, before purge_user_data; every deleteUser caller runs purgeAccount first) + src/lib/socialLogin.test.ts (iOS hands over the code). STILL NEEDED before it does anything live: the owner makes a Sign in with Apple key in the Apple developer account and sets APPLE_SIGNIN_PRIVATE_KEY + APPLE_SIGNIN_KEY_ID; until then both halves answer not_configured. Accounts that signed in with Apple before this ships have no stored token and cannot be revoked until they sign in again; web/Android Apple sign-in stores none (only iOS sends the code).** **2026-10-30 B84 (wip/b84-apple-revoke): apple-token-store now stores a token only when the id_token Apple returns from the same code exchange is for our client (iss appleid.apple.com, aud the client id) and its sub equals one of the CALLER's Apple identities (identity_data.sub; measured on prod: all 16 apple identities have it, equal to provider_id). A code from another Apple account answers 403 identity_mismatch and nothing is stored. GUARD: src/test/edge/apple-token-store.test.ts (the real handler through the edge harness, planted mismatch stores nothing).** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

- [~] **Q1876 MEDIUM Gift card is consumed at ACH checkout completion but not restored if the ACH debit bounces (ME-046 follow-up, Sentry bot finding on PR #3176, 2026-10-16).** `checkoutSessionCompleted` marks the gift card "redeemed" before detecting `isAsyncPayment`. If the ACH debit later bounces, `paymentIntentPaymentFailed` sets the job to "failed" but never calls `restore_gift_card_for_job`, leaving the donor's credit permanently spent on a job that was never funded. Only affects jobs that used a gift card for partial payment AND paid via ACH (US bank account). Fix: in `paymentIntentPaymentFailed`, when the job transitions to "failed" and `payment_method_types` includes "us_bank_account" (or `isAsyncPayment` is detectable), call `restore_gift_card_for_job` if a gift card was applied to the job. GUARD when built: a stripe-webhook test case with a gift-card-applied job and a `payment_intent.payment_failed` event that asserts the gift card is restored. **BUILT 2026-10-17 (sec-a, ready/sec-a-1017-2):** prod read-only first: 23 gift_cards, 0 redeemed, 0 redeemed on an unfunded job, so no recipient has lost a gift this way yet. Not restore_gift_card_for_job (per lh-money-escrow on 6b06413c7: its minted replacement is unique per job, so a failed job re-funded with it could never return the gift again, and the old 'redeemed' row stayed counted as gift escrow): new service-role RPC return_bounced_gift_card (migration 20261017074500) puts the SAME row back ('sent', no job, not redeemed, 90-day expiry floor) under the job's lock, only while the job's payment_status is 'failed', it has no open checkout and no payout_transfers row; a second call answers not_consumed; it returns the gift's payment_status so a gift whose donation was refunded is not called usable. paymentIntentPaymentFailed calls it only for pi.metadata.gift_card_id (create-payment's shortfall PaymentIntent), on the first delivery and on the retry after a throw (which now sends the notice the first delivery lost); a refusal pages money_at_risk; PGRST202 pages and lets the bounce stand. checkoutSessionCompleted's Q1417 re-claim now needs the job's CURRENT session, so a redelivered completion of the bounced checkout cannot spend the returned gift again. GUARD: src/test/edge/stripe-webhook.test.ts "Q1876 a bounced bank debit gives back the gift card" (10 new cases; 9 red on main's handlers, green after, 172/172; 8 @mutate killed) + PGlite ~/.lh-pglite/q1876.mjs (applied 3x, 20 checks, 6 planted mutants killed). lh-money-escrow on 2cd90f553: approve with nits (nits 3-6 fixed in the branch; points 1 and 2 filed as Q1985 and Q1986). [~] until the deployed webhook + function are read back live. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, built; the after-deploy read-back of the webhook and function is not recorded (no marker).** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: partly done.** Left: Read back the deployed webhook and function live, then tick.

- [ ] **Q1887 LOW complete-signup's no-session one-shot check is read-then-write, so two overlapping no-session calls both pass (lh-authz-rls review of a02d0cd80, 2026-10-17; code read).** A Q1810 re-send arriving while the first request still runs, or a racing stranger, both pass the terms_accepted_at read, and the avatar is upserted to a fixed key before the UPDATE, so the loser can replace the stored photo. Fix: on the no-session path add `.is("terms_accepted_at", null)` to the profiles UPDATE and answer zero rows with "Profile already completed. Please log in."; also add guard cases for a `Bearer <anon key>` caller with getUser failing and for a non-object body (401, not 500), and correct the "with NO bio ... runs again" bullet in src/pages/auth/signup/retrySignupCompletion.ts (origin/wip/lander-rest-1017, Q1810) once both land; Q1888 is fixed for back-to-back re-sends by the one-shot rule (only this overlap remains). GUARD when built: src/test/edge/complete-signup-no-session-fields.test.ts. **PROGRESS 2026-10-17 (sec-b, branch ready/sec-b-1017-6 on -5 8533128e8): the atomic part and both guard cases are FIXED.** The no-session path now TAKES the one-shot slot with one conditional UPDATE (`terms_accepted_at = now() WHERE user_id = $1 AND terms_accepted_at IS NULL`, returning the row) right after the read check and BEFORE the photo upload or any write; zero rows answers "Profile already completed. Please log in.", so a racing call uploads and writes nothing. A holder that does not finish (any answer but 200, or a throw) RELEASES it (terms_accepted_at back to NULL, keyed on its own stamp), so the member's own retry is not locked out; the completion's UPDATE overwrites the stamp with the consent time. Residual, by design: an overlapping re-send that loses the claim is told "already finished" even if the holder then fails; the member then finishes through Log In. GUARD: src/test/edge/complete-signup-no-session-fields.test.ts, a live-row double where an UPDATE lands only if its WHERE matches at that moment (src/test/edge/mocks/completeSignupWrites.ts): Promise.allSettled of two no-session calls that both pass the read -> [200, 403], one upload, one consent row, one referral (red on 8533128e8: [200, 200]); refused-after-claim releases and the retry completes; the release is keyed on its own stamp (a completion that landed but whose answer failed is never cleared); `Bearer <anon key>` with getUser failing is the no-session path; non-object bodies (null, string, array, number) are 401 with no write. From the lh-authz-rls review of a8e881b8c (APPROVE WITH NOTES): the 18+ gate now runs BEFORE the photo upload, so a refused no-session call uploads nothing (red on a8e881b8c). 4 new @mutate killed; all 26 registered on complete-signup still killed. Not done (review note c, optional, pre-existing window): the no-session completion UPDATE could also require the claim stamp. LEFT: the "with NO bio ... runs again" bullet in src/pages/auth/signup/retrySignupCompletion.ts, which exists only on origin/wip/lander-rest-1017 (Q1810), so it cannot be edited here; correct it once Q1810 is on main (a re-send now meets "Profile already completed" whether or not a bio was saved; an overlapping one loses the claim). finding: lh-authz-rls@a02d0cd80#1; finding: lh-authz-rls@a02d0cd80#3 STATUS 2026-10-17: the batch1 commit "chore(signup): name Q1887 in the complete-signup comments" only renamed the placeholder Q1899 to Q1887 in comments; the item is unchanged (what remains is the retrySignupCompletion.ts bullet, still on origin/wip/lander-rest-1017). **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, the item's own 10-17 status says unchanged; the remaining bullet sits on origin/wip/lander-rest-1017.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

Fix: decide which case is the real one and make the run match it. - [~] **Q1901 MEDIUM The loading-states request budget sits at one run's exact measurement, so the next run's noise fails it: perTest 2477 over its budget 2469 (loading-states-refresh 39008870955 step `request-budget.mjs --label loading-states`, 2026-10-17; 2469 is wip/reds-guards 6fd2dc8ee "budget at its measured value"; main still says 2116).** Busiest shapes in that run: `GET /storage/v1/object/public/avatars/:id/avatar.jpg` x197, `POST /rest/v1/rpc/feature_enabled_for` x119, `POST /rest/v1/rpc/get_my_pending_direct_offers` x114, `GET /rest/v1/profiles?select=*&user_id=eq.:id` x99. Fix: set the budget from at least two measured runs, with its headroom stated beside it, or cut the repeated reads (119 feature_enabled_for calls in one run is the first one to look at). Root cause cut (reds-1019-2, 2026-10-19): useFeature asked `feature_enabled_for` once PER SWITCH per cold load (39158661660: x480 over 118 signed-in boots, 3-6 a boot); every useFeature/useFeatureStatus now observes ONE batched query of `public.feature_switches_for_me(keys)` and selects its key (migration 20261019043416, SECURITY INVOKER over feature_enabled_for, granted to authenticated only; the per-switch read runs only while the batched RPC is missing, isMissingRpcNamed in src/lib/missingRpc.ts = PGRST202 with no hint or with a hint naming another function, which PostgREST sends for a missing function near an existing name; never persisted). Guards: src/hooks/useFeature.test.tsx "asks once per cold load" (red on the old hook: 4 requests for 5 reads), src/test/featureSwitchesOneRead.test.ts (client + newest migration definition), src/test/pglite/featureSwitchesForMe.pglite.mjs. Budget left at 2116 on purpose (lead review 2026-10-19): still owed, after it deploys, dispatch loading-states-refresh twice and write the higher MEASURED value with its run ids. Done when two consecutive loading-states-refresh runs pass the budget step (the nightly-red issue closes on a green run): done-when: issue #2601 closed **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, done-when issue #2601 is OPEN (gh 2026-10-21).** **SIGHTING 2026-10-25 (lane C, run logs): the request budgets are over because each page now reads more, not because a budget is stale.** loading-states 40125457759 perTest 4433 vs 2116 (about 144 copies each of get_jobs_for_my_applications, get_my_pending_direct_offers, user_violations, reviews, get_job_offer_targets, jobs: the whole activity load on every captured page); slow-network 39909180691 perTest 54.8 vs 35.8: against its last green run 39377441143 (bc2d2abfda, 2026-10-20) nine reads are new at about 25 each (get_job_offer_targets, get_jobs_for_my_applications, user_violations, reviews, jobs, applications x2, backout_notices, shop_orders), landed after it (e.g. 6a97616001 Home Coming up adds shop_orders + declutter bookings); chromium-anon-surface 40212626996 2.8 vs 1.8 (5 of 11 are the same `open_jobs_browse ... limit=4` read); abuse-journeys 3.6 vs 3.2 on 10-20 is already recalibrated to 3.6. Decide per label: trim the per-page reads or re-baseline with the measured run named. **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q2190 LOW 58 hand-written PGRST202 checks in app code read ANY PGRST202 as "not deployed yet", so an RPC whose signature changed falls back quietly instead of failing (lead review of wip/reds-1019-2, 2026-10-19; same class as Q2086).** PostgREST answers PGRST202 for a missing function AND for one that exists with other parameters; only the second carries a hint naming the function itself. The three callers of the lenient src/lib/missingRpc.ts helper (useSectionsOpen, applyRateLimit, threadMutes; all seven RPCs on prod, pg_proc 2026-10-19) now use isMissingRpcNamed(err, "<rpc>") and the lenient helper is gone (wip/reds-1019-13). Left: the 58 inline `code === "PGRST202"` / `!== "PGRST202"` comparisons (CancellationDialog, JobTracking, useUserProfileData, admin cards, ...): move each to isMissingRpcNamed with the RPC it called, lowering RAW_PGRST202_SITES in src/test/missingRpcIsNamed.test.ts by exactly that much in the same commit. GUARD: src/test/missingRpcIsNamed.test.ts (no lenient helper; every isMissingRpcNamed names an RPC its file calls; exact ratchet of raw comparisons). finding: lead@168e63884#1. done-when: test src/test/missingRpcIsNamed.test.ts with RAW_PGRST202_SITES = 0 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, src/test/missingRpcIsNamed.test.ts still has RAW_PGRST202_SITES = 58 and src/lib/missingRpc.ts still exists.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

- [ ] **Q1904 LOW A cancel that runs in the seconds between a checkout funding its job and settleOnboardingFee refunding a duplicate $2 setup fee returns those $2 twice (lh-money-escrow review of ba10093ae, Q1675 follow-up; code read, narrow window, not seen on prod).** checkoutSessionCompleted sets payment_status 'escrow' (~958), calls instant-job-match, and only then runs settleOnboardingFee (~1227); a cancel_escrow or void-cancelled-payments in that gap refunds the full ceiling (which still holds the duplicate $2), then settleOnboardingFee refunds the $2 again out of the card fee. Fix: settleOnboardingFee skips its refund (and says so) when the charge's amount_refunded already covers the escrow ceiling, or the duplicate refund runs before the escrow flip. Unverified whether Stripe Tax reverses a Checkout-taxed charge's tax on its own when a dispute is lost (its docs say it does not for the PaymentIntent-linked Tax API flow). - [ ] **Q1902 LOW Three loose ends of the Q1491 job payout hold (lh-money-escrow review of the Q1491 rebuild 7133adc73, 2026-10-15).** (a) The hold is upserted before the escrow write's funded-job guard, so a delivery that turns out to be a duplicate funding (refunded in full by that branch) leaves a hold on a properly funded job: fail-safe, but it blocks a legitimate payout and the "underfunded" page is wrong. Fix: upsert with `.select("job_id")` (a row only when this delivery inserted it) and delete that hold in the duplicate-funding branch. (b) money-reconciliation excludes only Helpr holds, so a job-held payout_pending job is reported as payout_pending_stranded: load job_payout_holds and report it as held. (c) process-scheduled-payouts finds a job hold only at the claim, so every run repeats the account and transfer-group reads and rolls back the onboarding fee for each held job: call checkJobPayoutHold beside the Helpr check and skip with PAYOUT_HELD_CODE. Also weigh: void-cancelled-payments' two fee transfers send unlinked (no source_transaction) when latest_charge is missing; the trigger DETAIL says a held job pays nobody. GUARD when built: a stripe-webhook edge case of a duplicate-funded delivery (no hold left), a money-reconciliation case (held, not stranded) and a process-scheduled-payouts case (no Stripe read for a held job). finding: lh-money-escrow@7133adc73#3 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

- [ ] **Q1920 LOW fixtureSchemaContract's literal scanner reads apostrophes in comments as string quotes, so one apostrophe added to a test file's comment can make it grade the whole file as one table row (found building Q1782 (2), 2026-10-15).** objectLiterals/balanced (src/test/helpers/schemaConstraints.ts) respects quotes but not comments: in src/test/edge/chargeRefundUpdated.test.ts an unbalanced brace in a header @mutate line plus one comment apostrophe turned a 9,537-character span (every test in the file) into one "recurring_visit_payments row" whose first status was a job's 'cancelled', a false red. Worked around in that file by rewording two comments; the scanner still has the hole (a parity flip can as easily HIDE a real bad fixture). Fix: run the source through blankComments (src/test/helpers/blankNonCode.ts) before objectLiterals. Done when: the scanner blanks comments and a test proves an apostrophe in a comment no longer changes which literals are graded. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q1912 LOW Prove in Stripe TEST mode that a FULL visit tax reversal can be undone and the visit reversed again (lh-money-escrow + lh-silent-failure reviews of Q1782 (2), 2026-10-15).** Q1782 (2) reverses a refund that returns the whole labor + tax with mode full (the lead's rule), and a refund that later fails undoes its reversal with a full reversal of that reversal transaction. Stripe documents undoing a PARTIAL reversal this way, not a full one, and nothing says a re-issued refund's own reversal is then accepted on the original (it may be refused as an over-reversal: that pages, so it is not silent, but the tax on money really returned would stay reported). Also capture Stripe's real error for a reused reversal reference (type, code, param, message) so referenceTaken() in stripe-webhook/handlers/_visitTaxReversal.ts matches it exactly, and the real idempotency-in-use error. Done when: one test-mode run (tax calculation, createFromCalculation, full reversal, full reversal of that reversal, a second reversal on the original) is recorded with its ids, and the test fixtures use the captured errors (or the code switches to partial flat_amount everywhere if Stripe refuses the cycle). finding: lh-money-escrow@994f077b2#1 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no test-mode run of the full-reversal cycle is recorded; the one commit naming it (e2b8f7d535) is a neighbouring tax fix, not this proof.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

- [ ] **Q1926 LOW On a late in-time Declutter payment (B41) a SELLER who blocked the buyer reads sold_elsewhere, so the platform pays the card fee, where the same block on an open checkout reads seller_backed_out and the seller pays (lh-money-escrow review of 905501cb2, finding 1, the part older than that commit; code read).** declutter_mark_order_paid path (b) in 20261017060455 (from 20261014224914) folds the seller's block into v_sale_ok and answers sold_elsewhere; path (c) picks seller_backed_out first. The buyer is refunded in full either way; only who absorbs the fee differs. The buyer-block half of the finding is fixed in the stack (seller_out judged on the items alone). Fix: pick (b)'s reason with (c)'s CASE, and decide the buyer-blocked case with the owner (a buyer_cancelled refund keeps the fee, which (b)'s "every cent" notice would then contradict). Same question for a BUYER who blocked a seller who is NOT out: (b) reads sold_elsewhere (platform pays), (c) reads buyer_cancelled (the card fee is kept) (same review, re-review note 2). GUARD when built: PGlite cases in src/test/pglite/declutterSellerOutPaysFee.pglite.mjs for a seller-blocked and a buyer-blocked late payment. finding: lh-money-escrow@905501cb2#1 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q1927 LOW A Declutter seller's card-fee debt and its 'Card fee owed' notice are booked before the Stripe refund runs, so a refund Stripe refuses for good still leaves the seller owing (lh-money-escrow review of 905501cb2, finding 3; code read).** declutter_record_refund writes declutter_seller_fee_debts inside the webhook's transaction, before executeRefund in supabase/functions/stripe-webhook/handlers/declutterCheckout.ts; when the refund gives up ("Settle it by hand") nothing cancels the debt. Same for seller_backed_out and the other seller-pays reasons. Fix: cancel or hold the debt when a refund gives up, and release it when a hand refund settles the row. GUARD when built: a PGlite case that a given-up refund leaves no live debt. finding: lh-money-escrow@905501cb2#3 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q1928 LOW Declutter seller-out fee debt: four gaps listed as open in docs/specs/declutter-auctions.md when owner answer 7 was built (2026-10-17).** (1) no way to waive a seller's fee debt when a ban or flag is overturned; (2) a debt booked from an ESTIMATED fee (Stripe balance transaction unreadable) is not marked as estimated; (3) a paid order whose seller goes out AFTER payment can still end as buyer_no_show / admin_no_fault, so the buyer bears the fee; (4) a seller who blocked the buyer and is also out is billed with no notice. Also for the owner: the debt is only taken from that seller's later Declutter payouts, so a banned seller's debt is usually never collected, and it is erased if the seller deletes the account (seller_id ON DELETE CASCADE). GUARD when built: a PGlite case per gap in src/test/pglite/declutterSellerOutPaysFee.pglite.mjs. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; the four gaps are open.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q2338 MEDIUM Declutter strikes do not survive an account deletion: declutter_strikes.user_id CASCADEs, so a member with strikes can delete and re-register clean (owner ruling 2026-10-22, Declutter terms #12: strikes stay tied to the verified ID; Phase B of Q2307).** Fix: at deletion, retain the member's active strikes keyed by profiles.identity_sha256 (the fingerprint retained_bans already uses; no other PII: count, expiry, reason code), and stripe-idv-webhook restores them when a new account verifies with the same fingerprint, next to enforce_retained_ban; the live Terms/Privacy say so (lead condition 2026-10-21). Done when a PGlite proof shows strikes come back on a re-verified fingerprint and a purge test shows they are retained, and the Terms line is live. **DEFERRED 2026-10-30 B84 (lead agreed): measured on prod (read-only): declutter_strikes holds 0 rows, 0 profiles have identity_sha256, feature switch identity_check = off and declutter = testers. With ID checks off no fingerprint exists to key strikes on, so a fingerprint-keyed retention would keep nothing and stripe-idv-webhook could restore nothing; nothing is at risk today. Build it when identity_check goes back on (still prod's declutter_strikes_user_id_fkey ON DELETE CASCADE).** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q1930 LOW Declutter delivery prices "road miles" as straight-line distance x 1.3, rounded up (declutter-delivery lane, 2026-10-16; spec B60).** The app has no server-side routing (drive time exists only on the device), so declutter_road_miles estimates. Across a river or a bayou the real drive can be far longer than 1.3x, so the Helpr is underpaid on those trips. Done when declutter_road_miles (one SQL function, the only place miles are measured) reads a routing service's distance, with the 1.3x estimate as the fallback, and a test pins a known river-crossing pair. **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, declutter_road_miles still estimates (no routing service); not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q1931 MEDIUM Declutter delivery: a Helpr payout or a delivery-fee refund that gives up after 12 tries leaves its queue with at most one alert and no re-raise (declutter-delivery lane, 2026-10-16; code read).** declutter-money posts one Slack alert on the try that reaches 12 for a Helpr payout, and a delivery-fee refund that THROWS records only a cron defect; after that helpr_task_payouts_due / declutter_delivery_refunds_due skip the row forever, while the buyer was told "your delivery fee is coming back". Seller refunds already have declutter_refund_gave_up_check (20261017055500). Two more parts of the same gap (lh-money-escrow and lh-silent-failure re-reviews of db0ce9b8b): a delivery-fee refund ops sends BY HAND is never recorded (declutter_record_hand_refund marks only declutter_refunds rows), so the stricter refunds_recorded_cents never counts it and a seller or Helpr payout on that charge gives up as "not whole"; and a Helpr payout that gave up (failed, attempts >= 12) blocks the Helpr's account deletion forever (accountPurge treats every failed payout as owed). Done when both queues have the same gave-up check (one error_logs ledger row per row per day while unsettled, plus a buyer / Helpr notice), proven red-before in a PGlite proof. finding: lh-silent-failure@88fa498a4#4 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **SIGHTING 2026-10-21 (lh-money-escrow review of Q1929, finding 4):** the same gap for delivery-pay RAISE refunds: declutter_delivery_raise_refunds_due drops a raise after 12 tries, and on the throwing path the last try only records a cron defect (no gave-up alert, no buyer notice, though the buyer was told the money is coming back); fix both queues together. **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

- [ ] **Q1935 LOW Declutter delivery: after the delivery fee is refunded on its own, a later seller-fault or no-fault refund still takes the card fee on the WHOLE payment (declutter-delivery lane, 2026-10-16; code read of declutter_close_paid_order).** It passes o.card_fee_cents (the fee on the total including the delivery fee) while refunding only total minus the reserved delivery fee, so the seller's debt (or the kept fee) includes card cost on money already given back. Done when the fee is prorated to (total - reserved) / total and refundsKeepTheCardFee pins it. finding: lh-money-escrow@88fa498a4#6 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [~] **Q1936 MEDIUM Declutter Add a Helpr: a refunded order leaves its paid setup job running and tells the buyer nothing about it. OWNER DECIDED 2026-10-17 (spec A10): on a SELLER-fault refund (fee_policy full_seller_pays_fee) the setup job is cancelled with no cancellation fee, the card fee is returned, and the seller owes it as a Declutter fee debt; a condition for declutter_add_helpr leaving testers. THE SELLER-FAULT CASE IS BUILT (2026-10-17, add-a-helpr v2: trg_declutter_refund_cancels_setup_jobs + void-cancelled-payments reads seller_fault_refund_id + declutter_setup_job_fee_debt; guards src/test/pglite/declutterAddAHelpr.pglite.mjs A1-A10 and src/test/edge/void-cancelled-payments.declutterSellerFault.test.ts); STILL OPEN: the buyer's notice on every other refund (declutter-add-a-helpr-v2 lane, 2026-10-17; code read: no refund path in supabase/migrations reads declutter_order_jobs, 20261017073146 is its only reader).** When a Declutter order with a linked, paid Helpr job (assemble / wall-mount / set up) is refunded (no-show, problem decided for the buyer, no-fault close), the job stays open and funded, and the order card's Add a Helpr panel disappears (declutter_setup_helpr answers item_not_found for a refunded order), so the buyer may pay a Helpr to set up an item they no longer have. The link moves no money, so nothing is charged twice, but nothing tells the buyer. Done when every path that sets declutter_orders.status = 'refunded' sends the buyer a notice naming each linked, funded, not-cancelled job with a link to cancel it under the normal cancel rules (never auto-cancelled: the Helpr may already be booked), proven red-before in a PGlite proof on the real Declutter schema. finding: lh-money-escrow@a98e0049d6#3 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, the seller-fault case is built; the buyer notice for the other refunds is not (no commit names it).** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: partly done.** Left: Tell the buyer about the paid setup job on non-seller-fault refunds.

- [ ] **Q1992 MEDIUM A crew refund's dedupe misses a ledger row it did not write itself, so after Stripe's 24-hour idempotency window a second real refund can go out (lh-money-escrow review of 52ba80829, #2; pre-existing; code read 2026-10-17, not measured).** process-scheduled-payouts refundUnfilledCrewShares finds its earlier refund by a payment_refunds row with the same job_id AND source (crew_unfilled_refund / crew_dispute_refund). Two ways that row is missing: (a) charge.refunded (stripe-webhook/handlers/chargeRefunded.ts l.339-350) books the same refund first, with job_id and source stripe_dashboard, and the crew upsert (onConflict stripe_refund_id, ignoreDuplicates) leaves that label; (b) the run dies between stripe.refunds.create and the ledger upsert. The next run then calls refunds.create again under the same idempotency key: within 24h Stripe returns the same refund (same amount) or refuses a changed amount; after 24h the key has expired and a SECOND real refund goes out. Prod: 0 crew refunds and 0 completed group jobs (2026-10-17), so nothing has happened yet. Fix: stamp the crew refund itself first (refunds.create sends no metadata today, index.ts ~l.842, so no Stripe refund says it is a crew one: add metadata { job_id, crew_source }); then, before refunding, also look for a live refund on the charge stamped with this job's crew source (stripe.refunds.list({ payment_intent })) or a payment_refunds row on the same stripe_payment_intent_id of source stripe_dashboard, and relabel a stripe_dashboard row to the crew source, as recordRefund does (Q1261 (6)) (lh-money-escrow delta review of 45ccc2384, #5). GUARD when built: a process-scheduled-payouts-crew case where the prior row is stripe_dashboard (and one with no row but a live stamped Stripe refund) that sends no second refund. finding: lh-money-escrow@52ba80829#2 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

- [ ] **Q1979 LOW The SQL late-cancel notices promise "about $X" that the payout floors to whole dollars, and can say "you'll be compensated" when $0 is sent (lh-money-escrow review of a2b4cab67, finding 1, 2026-10-17; code read).** poster_cancel_job (crew loop and solo notice) and settle_one_off_jobs_for_banned_account quote the Helpr's cut unrounded, but void-cancelled-payments sends roundPayoutDownCents of it and records under $1 as waived. Pre-existing on legacy jobs; the flat 5% + 70c (Q1840) widens the zero-pay band from fees up to $1.10 to fees up to $1.78 (e.g. a $10 crew of 2: share fee $1.25, notice 'about $0.49', $0 sent). Fix: floor the quoted cut to whole dollars like roundPayoutDownCents and, when it is $0, use the no-fee wording and type. GUARD when built: a PGlite case of a sub-$1.78 flat fee whose notice names no compensation, red on 20261017104746. finding: lh-money-escrow@a2b4cab67#1 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
- [ ] **Q1994 LOW A bank payment that settles on a job already refunded/cancelled/cancelling is acked with no alert (lh-money-escrow review of af0fbedd6, nit 2; pre-existing).** paymentIntentSucceeded only acts when the job is null/unpaid/failed/abandoned; any other payment_status falls through silently, and a settled ACH debit on a job whose payment_status is 'refunded', 'cancelled' or 'cancelling' is captured money with no escrow to hold it. Prod 2026-10-17: no such rows with a PaymentIntent. Fix: page money_at_risk when the job's payment_status is outside escrow/payout_pending/released and the promotable set (a redelivery for a card payment that was later refunded must not page: key it on the PI being an ACH one or on the refund ledger). GUARD when built: a stripe-webhook payment_intent.succeeded case on a 'refunded' job that asserts the page. finding: lh-money-escrow@af0fbedd6#2 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**

- [ ] **Q2006 LOW process-scheduled-payouts re-drives held tips and held chargeback clawback repays on the LIVE key for every row, whatever the job's Stripe mode (lh-money-escrow review of the tip fixture, 2026-10-17; code read).** supabase/functions/process-scheduled-payouts/index.ts keeps `stripes.clientFor("live")` (:75-80) only for redriveHeldTips and redriveHeldClawbackRepays (:1993-1999); heldTipRepay.ts repayOne calls transfers.create({destination}) on it (:329-352), and chargebackClawback.ts:850 does the same. A row written by a TEST-mode tip or clawback (newly reachable once helper-e2e holds its sanctioned test-mode account, Q2007, and is on a payout hold) is refused by Stripe: no money moves, but an owed row records a defect every hourly run, and a reversed row goes to failed with a CRITICAL "pay it by hand" page. Measured 2026-10-17: tip_hold_redrives 0 rows, payout_holds 0 rows, so not reachable today. Fix: resolve each row's client with stripes.clientForParties([row.helper_id]) (or skip and log a test-mode row under the live key), with an edge test that fails on the live-only client. finding: lh-money-escrow@9a2c1e875#1 **TRIAGE 2026-10-21: still applies, no commit on origin/main names it; not built.** **TRIAGE 2026-10-30: still applies.**
